# Lender registry evidence

Rendered by `tools/build_lender_registry.py` from `docs/lender-registry-ledger.json` (ledger `sha256:2b6ab097d29e7465584513bb9b6eac65a2d387242d7ba2206ff5d6e4922c6026`). Never edit by hand: change the ledger, then run `python -m tools.build_lender_registry --write`.

The ledger is curated outside the code (contract C10): the orchestrator's research workflow consolidates the research into one entity per company, checks each entity adversarially and runs a completeness critic. The code only validates the ledger and renders this page and the GENERATED block of `analysis/classification_registry.py` from it. This page holds no statement text, amounts, account numbers or customer names.

## Admission

- An admitted entity is in its group's table. It has at least one public source, and an independent second agent's verdict of upheld or corrected that confirms it (spec section 5).
- An entity under review is in `POTENTIAL_LENDER_ALIASES` (review, never counted), whatever its group. No entity of a table read only with context (credit cards, card issuers, bank names, premium financing) is under review.
- Since classification 5.3 (owner ruling 2026-09-27), the debits of a review entity of the vehicle, mortgage, lease or personal-loan group take that flag group, marked unconfirmed (`REVIEW_FLAG_ENTITIES`), unless its business type is unknown. Its credits keep the potential tier's exclusion.
- An entity awaiting an owner ruling, or rejected, is in no table. This page lists it with its group, its public source and where the researchers looked.
- Every payee the owner named in spec section 2 is admitted in its ruled group: the validator refuses a ledger in which one is not.
- An entity of the registry as it stood before this ledger changes place, or loses an alias, only with a reason: a sentence of its notes that starts `Move:` (spec section 9). The moves section shows that reason.
- A premium-finance alias equals an alias of a hand-kept insurer (`INSURANCE_PROVIDER_ALIASES`) only on the owner's ruling (`PREMIUM_FINANCE_INSURER_ALIASES`). The insurance rule reads the premium-finance table first, so rows that print that alias stay insurance and are also flagged premium financing, and no overlap with it awaits a ruling.
- An entity of that registry that the owner merged into a ledger entity (`MERGED_REGISTRY_ENTITIES`) is held by that entity under its name. The moves section shows the merge, and an alias the merge loses needs a `Move:` sentence in that entity's notes.
- An alias of that registry that the owner ruled names another company (`REATTRIBUTED_REGISTRY_ALIASES`) is held by that company's ledger entity, and the known-payee and potential tables read it as that entity or not at all. The moves section shows it beside the entity that held it, and it is not counted as lost.

Its registered name is Payability Commercial Factors, LLC, and its site presents the daily payouts as the seller's own marketplace earnings paid early rather than debt, which reads as receivables purchase (factoring), although it is also a registered sales-based financing provider in Virginia. For the owner: whether Payability stays a lender or becomes Factoring (both keep the payout credits out of revenue under D1; only the lender group counts it as a lender). Decision 2026-09-25 (owner delegated to the orchestrator): PAYABILITY's credits are the account holder's own sales settled by the platform, so they are revenue, not financing proceeds (PAYOUT_CREDIT_ENTITIES, an exception to D1 that S2 reads); its debits keep their group, and Payability stays a counted lender for its debits (not factoring). |

The two records may share one business_type (government_or_development_lender). |

Decision 2026-09-25 (owner delegated to the orchestrator): American First Finance, LLC's credits are the account holder's own sales settled by the platform, so they are revenue, not financing proceeds (PAYOUT_CREDIT_ENTITIES, an exception to D1 that S2 reads); its debits keep their group. |

S2's step-8 card rule reads these aliases only on a debit that also carries card or payment wording (contract C10); a row that prints the name alone stays unflagged, and credits are never read (D1). Same issuer as the credit_card_payment entity American Express [credit card payment], whose public evidence and confirming verdicts are reused verbatim (one entity has one group, so the bare spellings need their own entity). Precision test (2026-09-25): S2's rule order simulated with S1's matcher on a scratch rendering of this ledger, over the local payee index and over every cached debit row; an alias is kept only when every payee key it newly claims (no earlier rule and no known payee claims the row) is a card payment of this issuer. Kept: AMEX CARDS, the bill-payee name Canadian banks print for an American Express card: it newly claims 2 payee keys (21 rows) of the cache and 1 of the payee index, all Amex card bill payments. Dropped by the test: AMERICAN EXPRESS, a bare brand: besides Amex card ACH payments whose originator name and entry description print in separate fields, it takes American Express business-loan payments (entry description LOAN PMT, the product of the alternative-position entity American Express Loans) and outgoing wires that name American Express: 3 payee keys of the cache, none only card payments. Those card ACH payments stay unflagged until S2 reads the entry description or the owner rules. Dropped by the test: AMEX, a 4-character bare brand whose new rows are a phone payment to Amex's collections channel and a paper check converted to ACH, neither printing the Amex product: 2 payee keys, both unresolved. |

Dropped by the test: WELLS FARGO, a bare bank brand that takes wires to businesses that bank at Wells Fargo, another payee's web payments, a card-spend line and bill payments to Wells Fargo whose product (card, mortgage, auto or other loan) is not printed: 4 payee keys of the cache, none a card payment. Dropped by the test: WF, a 2-character abbreviation that takes Wells Fargo Direct Pay payments to other payees (one is rent), the rows of the entity Wells Fargo business credit autopay and payments whose product is not printed: 4 payee keys of the cache, none a card payment. |
| WEX Inc | card_issuer_context | fleet_card_issuer | r4 upheld; r2 upheld; r3 upheld; r5 upheld | admitted | WEX INC, WRIGHT EXPRESS, WEX BANK SECUREFUND, WEX BANK, WEX CANADA | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WEX_Inc | - | Filed in card_issuer_context: bare issuer names that S2's step-8 rule reads only when the row also carries card or payment wording. WEX Inc. (formerly Wright Express Corp.; WEX Bank issues the WEX Fleet Card and WEX administers the EFS fleet cards): fleet fuel-card payments, which the owner's ruling files under Credit cards. Every printed spelling (WEX INC FLEET DEBI, WRIGHT EXPRESS FLEET DEBI, WEX INC EFSLLC) lacks card-payment wording, so none can be a credit_card_payment alias (C10); the bare issuer names are kept here instead. Gap for the owner: the FLEET DEBI rows carry no card wording either, so neither route flags them today. WEX INC does not reach WEXFORD merchants. Consolidated (2026-09-25): WEX Bank (batch 27), WEX Canada (batch 26) merged into this entity as the same company (WEX Bank is WEX Inc.'s card-issuing bank and WEX Canada its Canadian business); carried over: aliases WEX BANK SECUREFUND, WEX BANK, WEX CANADA, with their evidence and verdicts. |

No corpus row shows it, and the one-word alias is the whole printed payee name, so it is kept; the S1 collision audit reviews any other KATAPULT key. Decision 2026-09-25 (owner delegated to the orchestrator): KATAPULT stays: its only corpus rows are one same-day ACH PULL debit and ACH IN credit of equal amount (two analyses of what looks like the same statement), no other payee prints the word, and the engineering-software namesake does not appear; the lease group never reads the credit, and an equal same-day pair is what the statement-level reversal-pair pass clears. |

## Floor plan (`FLOOR_PLAN_ALIASES`)

Personal loans (consumer loans) would fit only CreditStrong's consumer products (Revolv, MAGNUM, Instal), as batch 19 files Self Financial and Kikoff; CS Business, the product the CS BIZ rows name, is sold only to business entities (LLCs, corporations, partnerships). Either way it is a flag, never lender math. |

ruling until the decision below. MERCH PAD is a Canadian pre-authorized-debit originator short name; INVESTMENT, PLACEMENT, INV/PLA and PLAC are the bilingual Payments Canada category words around it. Six research rounds (r1 to r5 and final) point to Merchant Growth Ltd. (Vancouver; formerly Merchant Advance Capital), an MCA and term funder repaid by daily or weekly pre-authorized debits, and 151 of the 153 analyses show daily or weekly fixed debits, but no public source ties the short name MERCH PAD to Merchant Growth, so every verdict stayed unresolved. If a public source ever ties MERCH PAD to Merchant Growth, these spellings join the Merchant Growth entity as an admitted lender. MERCHANT GROWTH FUND, the long originator name printed on other Canadian statements, is not an alias here: no MERCH PAD row prints it, and it is a spelling of the Merchant Growth entity, whose alias MERCHANT GROWTH already matches it. Decision 2026-09-25 (owner delegated to the orchestrator): review tier, never counted: probably Merchant Growth, but unconfirmed, so its four measured spellings render into the potential table, which S2 reads on a debit after steps 1-10 and on a credit as a revenue exclusion. Measured on a scratch rendering: they claim 925 debit rows in 153 analyses of the local cache (5 payee keys; payee index 5 keys), every one this MERCH PAD originator, and no credit (the cache has none); they reach no other payee and not Merchant Growth's own spellings. Privacy 2026-09-26 (orchestrator ruling): a source or where-looked URL that named a private individual was removed. |

## Moves from the registry as it stood before this ledger

| ACH CAPITAL | confirmed_lender | no table | 1 | today's confirmed-lender entry is rejected and renders into no table. ACH Capital is a servicing and ACH-debit platform for alternative-finance companies, not a funder, and no statement in the corpus prints it: every match of the alias ACH CAPITAL is an ACH card payment to Capital One (ACH CAPITAL ONE ... PMT). S2 fact 5 forbids the alias on any rendered entity, so it is kept here as evidence only. |
| AFC FUNDING CORP | confirmed_lender | floor_plan_financing | 0 | the owner's 2026-09-24 amendment files inventory (floor-plan) lenders for dealers under Floor plan, and AFC Funding Corporation is the receivables-funding subsidiary of Automotive Finance Corporation (AFC, an OPENLANE company), a dealer floor-plan lender, so today's lender entry moves to floor_plan_financing; both of today's aliases stay. Decision 2026-09-25 (owner delegated to the orchestrator): stays floor_plan_financing (a dealer floor-plan lender's receivables-funding subsidiary); S2's fact 9 and its Task-0 line for 'AFC FUNDING CORP XPRESS PAY' now expect floor_plan_financing. XPRESS PAY is AFC's payment channel on the descriptor; bare AFC is not an alias. |

Added from statement spellings: AFFIRM CANADA, AFFIRMCARD (the Affirm Card, one token that bare AFFIRM does not reach) and SP AFF (Shop Pay Installments, which Affirm collects; the merchant name follows it). Credits keep today's financing-proceeds treatment (spec D2). |

Irregular ACH debits at a credit union from the originator CREDORA CAPITAL, about five per analysis with no fixed amount or cadence. Round 1 found only placeholder domains (a 2025 snapshot of credoracapital.com looks like a template 'bank' site) and said not to register it as a lender. Round 4, researching another key, found that Credora Capital is a d/b/a of Rockstone Funding Group Inc (Brooklyn), an admitted revenue-based funder in batch 5: a 2025 New York court record names 'Rockstone Funding Group Inc d/b/a Credora Capital' as plaintiff, and credoracapital.net sells revenue-based financing. That verifier (upheld, confirmed) named a credit-union CREDORA CAPITAL originator as the d/b/a but did not examine these rows. So the funder reading is now the likeliest and the group records it, but the irregular debits do not show an advance's fixed remittance and no verdict covers this originator, so it stays in the review tier. If the d/b/a is accepted for these rows, CREDORA CAPITAL belongs to the Rockstone Funding Group Inc entity, and this record becomes rejected as a separate entity. Credora is also an unrelated crypto credit-rating firm. The alias reaches no other payee in the local payee index. Decision 2026-09-25 (owner delegated to the orchestrator): Folded on public evidence: a 2025 New York court record names 'Rockstone Funding Group Inc d/b/a Credora Capital' as plaintiff and credoracapital.net sells revenue-based financing, and the corpus rows fit an MCA (varying remittances with returned-item fees and a credit from the originator). CREDORA CAPITAL moves to Rockstone Funding Group Inc (a counted lender); the crypto rating firm Credora never debits these accounts. |
